Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `int_sop_2` (precision INT19)
- computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
- input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
- output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `clk`, the block registers its operands (`ax`, `ay`, `bx`, `by`) at one clock edge and `result` at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in `result` after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. `chainin` is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches `result` 2 cycles after it is applied. `chainout` is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value `result` loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before `result` shows it.
- reset: `reset` is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
- power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where `reset` is low, so, for a block whose inputs, `chainin` included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
- tie-off: `chainin` = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's `chainout` or a constant may drive it); `mode_sigs` = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. `chainout` reaches only the next block's `chainin`, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.

9 call(s) in 3 passes, in order

Role: planner plan reviewer editor acceptance reviewer. A plan reviewer sending the plan back, or the editor being asked again, is a retry inside the same pass.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, raygentop.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or nothing left to try. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: raygentop.v

response

I'll start by reading the design source around the candidate regions.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three 3-product sums of module bilinearintrp: r (2874/2875/2876), g (2877/2878/2879), b (2880/2881/2882)
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • sop_r: ax = {10'b0, u} (the mux output, 8 bits), ay = {12'b0, (ru - rw)} (7-bit wrapping subtract of the module inputs), bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, (rv - rw)}, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk; result -> res_r, chainout left unconnected. Output: assign r = res_r[6:0] - rwl2;
  • sop_g: ax = {10'b0, u}, ay = {12'b0, (gu - gw)}, bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, (gv - gw)}, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk; result -> res_g. Output: assign g = res_g[6:0] - gwl2;
  • sop_b: ax = {10'b0, u}, ay = {12'b0, (bu - bw)}, bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, (bv - bw)}, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk; result -> res_b. Output: assign b = res_b[6:0] - bwl2;
  • rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 are rw/gw/bw delayed two clock edges (the existing rwl/gwl/bwl registers plus one new 7-bit register each).

PLAN:

1. What the original computes (raygentop.v:2818-2886).
u,v are an 8-bit combinational mux of the module inputs (lines 2818-2858). At each posedge: ul<=u, vl<=v, wl<=8'hFF-u-v (8-bit, wrapping), rul<=ru … bwl<=bw (line 2862-2873); at the same posedge the nine products of the previously registered values are loaded into i1r…i3b (lines 2874-2882). Outputs are assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) etc. (2884-2886) with r,g,b declared [6:0] (2786-2791) — only the low 7 bits of each 3-product sum exist. So, with inputs sampled at edge k, r after edge k+1 is
(ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl) mod 128.

2. The regrouping (why 3 blocks cover 9 products).
wl = (255 - u - v) mod 256, so wl*rwl ≡ (255-ul-vl)*rwl (mod 128) — the discarded multiple of 256 is a multiple of 128. Since 255 ≡ -1 (mod 128):

r ≡ ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl (mod 128)

and the same for g,b. Congruence mod 128 survives multiplication, so the differences may be formed as 7-bit wrapping subtracts of the raw inputs (ru-rw, etc.). Each colour therefore needs exactly two products — one block — plus one 7-bit subtract outside. The third product of each colour is folded into the block's operands, not dropped. Worked check: u=200,v=100,ru=10,rv=20,rw=30 → original wl=211, sum=10330, r=90; rewrite dru=108, drv=118, block result=33400, 120-30 = 90. u=v=ru=rv=0, rw=127 → original 32385 mod 128 = 1; rewrite 0-127 mod 128 = 1.

No overflow anywhere: each block product ≤ 255*127 = 32385, the block sum ≤ 64770, far below 2^37, so result[6:0] is exactly the sum mod 128. No ASSUMES: line is needed — the identity holds for every input value, including the testbench's full-width random stimulus.

3. Registers / timing. Before the rewrite: module input → stage 1 (ul/vl/wl/rul…bwl) → stage 2 (the product registers i1r…i3b) → combinational sum → output: 2 register stages, products registered 1 stage before the (combinational) sum. After the rewrite: the block's input register replaces stage 1 (it registers u, v and the precomputed differences), and the block's result register replaces the product registers and performs the sum: 2 register stages, with the -rwl2 correction combinational after the second stage. Cycle-for-cycle identical for all nine regions. The correction term needs rw aligned with the block result, i.e. delayed two edges, hence the extra rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 registers.

4. Edits to make in /work/raygentop.v, module bilinearintrp only.

  • Keep the port list, the port declarations, the reg[7:0] u,v declarations and the whole combinational mux always block (2818-2858) unchanged.
  • Delete the register declarations ul, vl, wl, i1b…i3r, rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl (lines 2796-2807, 2808-2809, 2811-2812, 2814-2815) — they become dead. Keep rwl, gwl, bwl (2810, 2813, 2816) and add reg[6:0] rwl2, gwl2, bwl2;.
  • Replace the clocked block (2860-2883) with just:
    always @(posedge clk) begin rwl <= rw; gwl <= gw; bwl <= bw; rwl2 <= rwl; gwl2 <= gwl; bwl2 <= bwl; end
  • Add the six difference wires: wire [6:0] dru = ru - rw; wire [6:0] drv = rv - rw; wire [6:0] dgu = gu - gw; wire [6:0] dgv = gv - gw; wire [6:0] dbu = bu - bw; wire [6:0] dbv = bv - bw; (7-bit wrapping subtracts — do not widen them).
  • Add wire [36:0] res_r, res_g, res_b; and three int_sop_2 instances exactly as in MAPPING, connecting every input port by name, chainin tied to 37'b0, mode_sigs to 12'b0, reset to 1'b0. Leave each chainout unconnected (.chainout()); nothing may read it, and the blocks are not chained.
  • Replace lines 2884-2886 with assign r = res_r[6:0] - rwl2; assign g = res_g[6:0] - gwl2; assign b = res_b[6:0] - bwl2; (7-bit arithmetic on both sides).
  • Do not add a definition of int_sop_2 to raygentop.v (the behavioural model is sim_reference/int_sop_2.v), and change nothing outside module bilinearintrp — in particular leave matmult (lines 335-343) alone; its multiplies are not candidates.

5. Reset / start-up. bilinearintrp has no reset port and the testbench drives none (tb_equiv_bilin.v line 5), so every block's reset is tied low; the blocks load every edge. All block inputs are defined from time 0 (the mux defaults to u=v=0, line 2852-2856), so every block output and the new rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 registers are defined and zero from the second edge, well inside the 8-cycle lead-in before comparison starts. Nothing in the rewrite relies on a power-up value.

6. Candidates left: none. All nine products are converted; six go onto block ports directly, three (2876, 2879, 2882 — the wl* products) are folded into the block operands with a 7-bit correction outside, which is the regrouping that makes T2 apply. Three blocks (3 of 132 DSP tiles) replace nine 8×7 multipliers, nine 15-bit product registers, six 15-bit adders and eight 7/8-bit stage-1 registers; the soft logic added is six 7-bit subtracts, three 7-bit subtracts and three 7-bit registers.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is raygentop.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source around the candidate regions.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three 3-product sums of module bilinearintrp: r (2874/2875/2876), g (2877/2878/2879), b (2880/2881/2882)
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • sop_r: ax = {10'b0, u} (the mux output, 8 bits), ay = {12'b0, (ru - rw)} (7-bit wrapping subtract of the module inputs), bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, (rv - rw)}, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk; result -> res_r, chainout left unconnected. Output: assign r = res_r[6:0] - rwl2;
  • sop_g: ax = {10'b0, u}, ay = {12'b0, (gu - gw)}, bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, (gv - gw)}, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk; result -> res_g. Output: assign g = res_g[6:0] - gwl2;
  • sop_b: ax = {10'b0, u}, ay = {12'b0, (bu - bw)}, bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, (bv - bw)}, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk; result -> res_b. Output: assign b = res_b[6:0] - bwl2;
  • rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 are rw/gw/bw delayed two clock edges (the existing rwl/gwl/bwl registers plus one new 7-bit register each).

PLAN:

1. What the original computes (raygentop.v:2818-2886).
u,v are an 8-bit combinational mux of the module inputs (lines 2818-2858). At each posedge: ul<=u, vl<=v, wl<=8'hFF-u-v (8-bit, wrapping), rul<=ru … bwl<=bw (line 2862-2873); at the same posedge the nine products of the previously registered values are loaded into i1r…i3b (lines 2874-2882). Outputs are assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) etc. (2884-2886) with r,g,b declared [6:0] (2786-2791) — only the low 7 bits of each 3-product sum exist. So, with inputs sampled at edge k, r after edge k+1 is
(ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl) mod 128.

2. The regrouping (why 3 blocks cover 9 products).
wl = (255 - u - v) mod 256, so wl*rwl ≡ (255-ul-vl)*rwl (mod 128) — the discarded multiple of 256 is a multiple of 128. Since 255 ≡ -1 (mod 128):

r ≡ ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl (mod 128)

and the same for g,b. Congruence mod 128 survives multiplication, so the differences may be formed as 7-bit wrapping subtracts of the raw inputs (ru-rw, etc.). Each colour therefore needs exactly two products — one block — plus one 7-bit subtract outside. The third product of each colour is folded into the block's operands, not dropped. Worked check: u=200,v=100,ru=10,rv=20,rw=30 → original wl=211, sum=10330, r=90; rewrite dru=108, drv=118, block result=33400, 120-30 = 90. u=v=ru=rv=0, rw=127 → original 32385 mod 128 = 1; rewrite 0-127 mod 128 = 1.

No overflow anywhere: each block product ≤ 255*127 = 32385, the block sum ≤ 64770, far below 2^37, so result[6:0] is exactly the sum mod 128. No ASSUMES: line is needed — the identity holds for every input value, including the testbench's full-width random stimulus.

3. Registers / timing. Before the rewrite: module input → stage 1 (ul/vl/wl/rul…bwl) → stage 2 (the product registers i1r…i3b) → combinational sum → output: 2 register stages, products registered 1 stage before the (combinational) sum. After the rewrite: the block's input register replaces stage 1 (it registers u, v and the precomputed differences), and the block's result register replaces the product registers and performs the sum: 2 register stages, with the -rwl2 correction combinational after the second stage. Cycle-for-cycle identical for all nine regions. The correction term needs rw aligned with the block result, i.e. delayed two edges, hence the extra rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 registers.

4. Edits to make in /work/raygentop.v, module bilinearintrp only.

  • Keep the port list, the port declarations, the reg[7:0] u,v declarations and the whole combinational mux always block (2818-2858) unchanged.
  • Delete the register declarations ul, vl, wl, i1b…i3r, rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl (lines 2796-2807, 2808-2809, 2811-2812, 2814-2815) — they become dead. Keep rwl, gwl, bwl (2810, 2813, 2816) and add reg[6:0] rwl2, gwl2, bwl2;.
  • Replace the clocked block (2860-2883) with just:
    always @(posedge clk) begin rwl <= rw; gwl <= gw; bwl <= bw; rwl2 <= rwl; gwl2 <= gwl; bwl2 <= bwl; end
  • Add the six difference wires: wire [6:0] dru = ru - rw; wire [6:0] drv = rv - rw; wire [6:0] dgu = gu - gw; wire [6:0] dgv = gv - gw; wire [6:0] dbu = bu - bw; wire [6:0] dbv = bv - bw; (7-bit wrapping subtracts — do not widen them).
  • Add wire [36:0] res_r, res_g, res_b; and three int_sop_2 instances exactly as in MAPPING, connecting every input port by name, chainin tied to 37'b0, mode_sigs to 12'b0, reset to 1'b0. Leave each chainout unconnected (.chainout()); nothing may read it, and the blocks are not chained.
  • Replace lines 2884-2886 with assign r = res_r[6:0] - rwl2; assign g = res_g[6:0] - gwl2; assign b = res_b[6:0] - bwl2; (7-bit arithmetic on both sides).
  • Do not add a definition of int_sop_2 to raygentop.v (the behavioural model is sim_reference/int_sop_2.v), and change nothing outside module bilinearintrp — in particular leave matmult (lines 335-343) alone; its multiplies are not candidates.

5. Reset / start-up. bilinearintrp has no reset port and the testbench drives none (tb_equiv_bilin.v line 5), so every block's reset is tied low; the blocks load every edge. All block inputs are defined from time 0 (the mux defaults to u=v=0, line 2852-2856), so every block output and the new rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 registers are defined and zero from the second edge, well inside the 8-cycle lead-in before comparison starts. Nothing in the rewrite relies on a power-up value.

6. Candidates left: none. All nine products are converted; six go onto block ports directly, three (2876, 2879, 2882 — the wl* products) are folded into the block operands with a 7-bit correction outside, which is the regrouping that makes T2 apply. Three blocks (3 of 132 DSP tiles) replace nine 8×7 multipliers, nine 15-bit product registers, six 15-bit adders and eight 7/8-bit stage-1 registers; the soft logic added is six 7-bit subtracts, three 7-bit subtracts and three 7-bit registers.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/.

No reset port in bilinearintrp; tb_equiv_bilin.v:5 confirms "Resets: none".
  • Visibility. Grep confirms ul, vl, wl, i1r…i3b, rul…bwl have no reader outside lines 2862-2886 inside bilinearintrp (the ul/vl at 2706-2756 are a different module's regs, untouched by a bilinearintrp-only edit). u,v stay outside the block as block-port drivers.
  • Arithmetic. wl ≡ −1−u−v (mod 128) because the 8-bit wrap is a multiple of 128, so ul*ru+vl*rv+wl*rw ≡ u*(ru−rw)+v*(rv−rw)−rw (mod 128); the 7-bit wrapping differences preserve the congruence through multiplication, and the final 15-bit adder truncation to 7 bits is also mod 128. Both worked examples recompute correctly (90 and 1). Max block sum 64770 ≪ 2³⁷, so result[6:0] is the exact residue. Holds for all inputs — no ASSUMES: needed, matching the tb's full-width random stimulus.
  • Timing. Original: inputs → stage-1 regs → product regs → combinational sum, 2 stages. Rewrite: block input register → block result register, 2 stages, with −rwl2 combinational after; rwl2 is rw delayed two edges, aligning with the result. Cycle-for-cycle identical.
  • Block contract. All input ports connected by name, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0 (design has none), chainout left unconnected and unread; 3 blocks of 132 DSP tiles; no product-term port unused.
  • Power-up. Block regs and new rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 are X for at most 2 edges; tb LEAD_IN=8 ticks precede cmp=1 (tb lines 217-219), so nothing relies on an initial value.
  • Required statements. All nine candidates accounted for (all converted, section 6); block count stated (3); register stages before/after stated for the converted regions.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule

prompt
Task

Edit raygentop.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Regions in the approved plan: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356. The plan names their shared sum as 'the three 3-product sums of module bilinearintrp: r (2874/2875/2876), g (2877/2878/2879), b (2880/2881/2882)'. In the design their products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh raygentop.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source around the candidate regions.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three 3-product sums of module bilinearintrp: r (2874/2875/2876), g (2877/2878/2879), b (2880/2881/2882)
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • sop_r: ax = {10'b0, u} (the mux output, 8 bits), ay = {12'b0, (ru - rw)} (7-bit wrapping subtract of the module inputs), bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, (rv - rw)}, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk; result -> res_r, chainout left unconnected. Output: assign r = res_r[6:0] - rwl2;
  • sop_g: ax = {10'b0, u}, ay = {12'b0, (gu - gw)}, bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, (gv - gw)}, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk; result -> res_g. Output: assign g = res_g[6:0] - gwl2;
  • sop_b: ax = {10'b0, u}, ay = {12'b0, (bu - bw)}, bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, (bv - bw)}, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk; result -> res_b. Output: assign b = res_b[6:0] - bwl2;
  • rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 are rw/gw/bw delayed two clock edges (the existing rwl/gwl/bwl registers plus one new 7-bit register each).

PLAN:

1. What the original computes (raygentop.v:2818-2886).
u,v are an 8-bit combinational mux of the module inputs (lines 2818-2858). At each posedge: ul<=u, vl<=v, wl<=8'hFF-u-v (8-bit, wrapping), rul<=ru … bwl<=bw (line 2862-2873); at the same posedge the nine products of the previously registered values are loaded into i1r…i3b (lines 2874-2882). Outputs are assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) etc. (2884-2886) with r,g,b declared [6:0] (2786-2791) — only the low 7 bits of each 3-product sum exist. So, with inputs sampled at edge k, r after edge k+1 is
(ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl) mod 128.

2. The regrouping (why 3 blocks cover 9 products).
wl = (255 - u - v) mod 256, so wl*rwl ≡ (255-ul-vl)*rwl (mod 128) — the discarded multiple of 256 is a multiple of 128. Since 255 ≡ -1 (mod 128):

r ≡ ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl (mod 128)

and the same for g,b. Congruence mod 128 survives multiplication, so the differences may be formed as 7-bit wrapping subtracts of the raw inputs (ru-rw, etc.). Each colour therefore needs exactly two products — one block — plus one 7-bit subtract outside. The third product of each colour is folded into the block's operands, not dropped. Worked check: u=200,v=100,ru=10,rv=20,rw=30 → original wl=211, sum=10330, r=90; rewrite dru=108, drv=118, block result=33400, 120-30 = 90. u=v=ru=rv=0, rw=127 → original 32385 mod 128 = 1; rewrite 0-127 mod 128 = 1.

No overflow anywhere: each block product ≤ 255*127 = 32385, the block sum ≤ 64770, far below 2^37, so result[6:0] is exactly the sum mod 128. No ASSUMES: line is needed — the identity holds for every input value, including the testbench's full-width random stimulus.

3. Registers / timing. Before the rewrite: module input → stage 1 (ul/vl/wl/rul…bwl) → stage 2 (the product registers i1r…i3b) → combinational sum → output: 2 register stages, products registered 1 stage before the (combinational) sum. After the rewrite: the block's input register replaces stage 1 (it registers u, v and the precomputed differences), and the block's result register replaces the product registers and performs the sum: 2 register stages, with the -rwl2 correction combinational after the second stage. Cycle-for-cycle identical for all nine regions. The correction term needs rw aligned with the block result, i.e. delayed two edges, hence the extra rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 registers.

4. Edits to make in /work/raygentop.v, module bilinearintrp only.

  • Keep the port list, the port declarations, the reg[7:0] u,v declarations and the whole combinational mux always block (2818-2858) unchanged.
  • Delete the register declarations ul, vl, wl, i1b…i3r, rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl (lines 2796-2807, 2808-2809, 2811-2812, 2814-2815) — they become dead. Keep rwl, gwl, bwl (2810, 2813, 2816) and add reg[6:0] rwl2, gwl2, bwl2;.
  • Replace the clocked block (2860-2883) with just:
    always @(posedge clk) begin rwl <= rw; gwl <= gw; bwl <= bw; rwl2 <= rwl; gwl2 <= gwl; bwl2 <= bwl; end
  • Add the six difference wires: wire [6:0] dru = ru - rw; wire [6:0] drv = rv - rw; wire [6:0] dgu = gu - gw; wire [6:0] dgv = gv - gw; wire [6:0] dbu = bu - bw; wire [6:0] dbv = bv - bw; (7-bit wrapping subtracts — do not widen them).
  • Add wire [36:0] res_r, res_g, res_b; and three int_sop_2 instances exactly as in MAPPING, connecting every input port by name, chainin tied to 37'b0, mode_sigs to 12'b0, reset to 1'b0. Leave each chainout unconnected (.chainout()); nothing may read it, and the blocks are not chained.
  • Replace lines 2884-2886 with assign r = res_r[6:0] - rwl2; assign g = res_g[6:0] - gwl2; assign b = res_b[6:0] - bwl2; (7-bit arithmetic on both sides).
  • Do not add a definition of int_sop_2 to raygentop.v (the behavioural model is sim_reference/int_sop_2.v), and change nothing outside module bilinearintrp — in particular leave matmult (lines 335-343) alone; its multiplies are not candidates.

5. Reset / start-up. bilinearintrp has no reset port and the testbench drives none (tb_equiv_bilin.v line 5), so every block's reset is tied low; the blocks load every edge. All block inputs are defined from time 0 (the mux defaults to u=v=0, line 2852-2856), so every block output and the new rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 registers are defined and zero from the second edge, well inside the 8-cycle lead-in before comparison starts. Nothing in the rewrite relies on a power-up value.

6. Candidates left: none. All nine products are converted; six go onto block ports directly, three (2876, 2879, 2882 — the wl* products) are folded into the block operands with a 7-bit correction outside, which is the regrouping that makes T2 apply. Three blocks (3 of 132 DSP tiles) replace nine 8×7 multipliers, nine 15-bit product registers, six 15-bit adders and eight 7/8-bit stage-1 registers; the soft logic added is six 7-bit subtracts, three 7-bit subtracts and three 7-bit registers.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

What changed (module bilinearintrp, raygentop.v:2762-2894 only)

The nine products were regrouped onto three blocks using wl = (255-u-v) mod 256 ≡ -1-u-v (mod 128), which holds exactly because r/g/b are only 7 bits:
r ≡ ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl (mod 128).

Region by region:

  • 2874 (i1r <= ul*rul) and 2875 (i2r <= vl*rvl) → the two products of sop_r, with ay/by carrying the differences dru = ru-rw, drv = rv-rw instead of the raw ru/rv.
  • 2876 (i3r <= wl*rwl) → folded into those same two operands plus the combinational correction - rwl2 on the output; no product of the original is dropped.
  • 2877/2878 → sop_g's two products (dgu, dgv); 2879 → folded, corrected by - gwl2.
  • 2880/2881 → sop_b's two products (dbu, dbv); 2882 → folded, corrected by - bwl2.

Supporting edits:

  • Deleted the now-dead ul, vl, wl, i1r…i3b, rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl declarations and the whole product/stage-1 clocked body; kept rwl/gwl/bwl and added rwl2/gwl2/bwl2, so the clocked block is now only the two-edge delay of rw/gw/bw that aligns the correction with the block result.
  • Added the six 7-bit wrapping difference wires and res_r/res_g/res_b (37-bit).
  • Three int_sop_2 instances, every input port connected by name: ax={10'b0,u}, bx={10'b0,v}, ay/by zero-extended differences, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; chainout left unconnected (blocks not chained, nothing reads it).
  • Outputs are now assign r = res_r[6:0] - rwl2; and likewise for g, b.

Register stages are unchanged at 2 (block input register replaces stage 1; block result register replaces the product registers and performs the sum), so every output is cycle-for-cycle identical. No overflow: each block sum ≤ 64770, far below 2^37. No ASSUMES: line — the identity holds for every input value. Nothing outside bilinearintrp was touched, and int_sop_2 is not defined in the design.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the

7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule
51

1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
2	# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
3	# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
4	# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
5	# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
6	# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
7	# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
8	# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
9	# of the target block's name fails this check.
10	# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
11	# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
12	# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
13	# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
14	set -u
15	f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
16	here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
17	lib=""
18	[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
19	[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
20	target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
21	if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
22	  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
23	  exit 1
24	fi
25	out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
26	rc=$?
27	if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
28	  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
29	  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
30	  echo "$out" | tail -10
31	  exit 1
32	fi
33	echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
34	echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
35	echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
36	                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
37	                   on {on=0}
38	                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
39	  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

prompt
Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The file raygentop.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.

The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source around the candidate regions.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three 3-product sums of module bilinearintrp: r (2874/2875/2876), g (2877/2878/2879), b (2880/2881/2882)
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • sop_r: ax = {10'b0, u} (the mux output, 8 bits), ay = {12'b0, (ru - rw)} (7-bit wrapping subtract of the module inputs), bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, (rv - rw)}, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk; result -> res_r, chainout left unconnected. Output: assign r = res_r[6:0] - rwl2;
  • sop_g: ax = {10'b0, u}, ay = {12'b0, (gu - gw)}, bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, (gv - gw)}, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk; result -> res_g. Output: assign g = res_g[6:0] - gwl2;
  • sop_b: ax = {10'b0, u}, ay = {12'b0, (bu - bw)}, bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, (bv - bw)}, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk; result -> res_b. Output: assign b = res_b[6:0] - bwl2;
  • rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 are rw/gw/bw delayed two clock edges (the existing rwl/gwl/bwl registers plus one new 7-bit register each).

PLAN:

1. What the original computes (raygentop.v:2818-2886).
u,v are an 8-bit combinational mux of the module inputs (lines 2818-2858). At each posedge: ul<=u, vl<=v, wl<=8'hFF-u-v (8-bit, wrapping), rul<=ru … bwl<=bw (line 2862-2873); at the same posedge the nine products of the previously registered values are loaded into i1r…i3b (lines 2874-2882). Outputs are assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) etc. (2884-2886) with r,g,b declared [6:0] (2786-2791) — only the low 7 bits of each 3-product sum exist. So, with inputs sampled at edge k, r after edge k+1 is
(ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl) mod 128.

2. The regrouping (why 3 blocks cover 9 products).
wl = (255 - u - v) mod 256, so wl*rwl ≡ (255-ul-vl)*rwl (mod 128) — the discarded multiple of 256 is a multiple of 128. Since 255 ≡ -1 (mod 128):

r ≡ ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl (mod 128)

and the same for g,b. Congruence mod 128 survives multiplication, so the differences may be formed as 7-bit wrapping subtracts of the raw inputs (ru-rw, etc.). Each colour therefore needs exactly two products — one block — plus one 7-bit subtract outside. The third product of each colour is folded into the block's operands, not dropped. Worked check: u=200,v=100,ru=10,rv=20,rw=30 → original wl=211, sum=10330, r=90; rewrite dru=108, drv=118, block result=33400, 120-30 = 90. u=v=ru=rv=0, rw=127 → original 32385 mod 128 = 1; rewrite 0-127 mod 128 = 1.

No overflow anywhere: each block product ≤ 255*127 = 32385, the block sum ≤ 64770, far below 2^37, so result[6:0] is exactly the sum mod 128. No ASSUMES: line is needed — the identity holds for every input value, including the testbench's full-width random stimulus.

3. Registers / timing. Before the rewrite: module input → stage 1 (ul/vl/wl/rul…bwl) → stage 2 (the product registers i1r…i3b) → combinational sum → output: 2 register stages, products registered 1 stage before the (combinational) sum. After the rewrite: the block's input register replaces stage 1 (it registers u, v and the precomputed differences), and the block's result register replaces the product registers and performs the sum: 2 register stages, with the -rwl2 correction combinational after the second stage. Cycle-for-cycle identical for all nine regions. The correction term needs rw aligned with the block result, i.e. delayed two edges, hence the extra rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 registers.

4. Edits to make in /work/raygentop.v, module bilinearintrp only.

  • Keep the port list, the port declarations, the reg[7:0] u,v declarations and the whole combinational mux always block (2818-2858) unchanged.
  • Delete the register declarations ul, vl, wl, i1b…i3r, rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl (lines 2796-2807, 2808-2809, 2811-2812, 2814-2815) — they become dead. Keep rwl, gwl, bwl (2810, 2813, 2816) and add reg[6:0] rwl2, gwl2, bwl2;.
  • Replace the clocked block (2860-2883) with just:
    always @(posedge clk) begin rwl <= rw; gwl <= gw; bwl <= bw; rwl2 <= rwl; gwl2 <= gwl; bwl2 <= bwl; end
  • Add the six difference wires: wire [6:0] dru = ru - rw; wire [6:0] drv = rv - rw; wire [6:0] dgu = gu - gw; wire [6:0] dgv = gv - gw; wire [6:0] dbu = bu - bw; wire [6:0] dbv = bv - bw; (7-bit wrapping subtracts — do not widen them).
  • Add wire [36:0] res_r, res_g, res_b; and three int_sop_2 instances exactly as in MAPPING, connecting every input port by name, chainin tied to 37'b0, mode_sigs to 12'b0, reset to 1'b0. Leave each chainout unconnected (.chainout()); nothing may read it, and the blocks are not chained.
  • Replace lines 2884-2886 with assign r = res_r[6:0] - rwl2; assign g = res_g[6:0] - gwl2; assign b = res_b[6:0] - bwl2; (7-bit arithmetic on both sides).
  • Do not add a definition of int_sop_2 to raygentop.v (the behavioural model is sim_reference/int_sop_2.v), and change nothing outside module bilinearintrp — in particular leave matmult (lines 335-343) alone; its multiplies are not candidates.

5. Reset / start-up. bilinearintrp has no reset port and the testbench drives none (tb_equiv_bilin.v line 5), so every block's reset is tied low; the blocks load every edge. All block inputs are defined from time 0 (the mux defaults to u=v=0, line 2852-2856), so every block output and the new rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 registers are defined and zero from the second edge, well inside the 8-cycle lead-in before comparison starts. Nothing in the rewrite relies on a power-up value.

6. Candidates left: none. All nine products are converted; six go onto block ports directly, three (2876, 2879, 2882 — the wl* products) are folded into the block operands with a 7-bit correction outside, which is the regrouping that makes T2 apply. Three blocks (3 of 132 DSP tiles) replace nine 8×7 multipliers, nine 15-bit product registers, six 15-bit adders and eight 7/8-bit stage-1 registers; the soft logic added is six 7-bit subtracts, three 7-bit subtracts and three 7-bit registers.

ACCEPT

Checks performed:

Plan conformance. The edit matches the approved plan region-for-region: declarations ul,vl,wl,i1*..i3*,rul,rvl,gul,gvl,bul,bvl deleted (now dead), rwl/gwl/bwl kept plus new rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 (raygentop.v:2796-2801), six 7-bit difference wires (2806-2811), three int_sop_2 instances (2870-2889), and the three output assigns (2891-2893). The clocked block (2860-2868) is exactly the two-stage rw/gw/bw delay the plan specifies. The log's subtree check confirms nothing outside bilinearintrp changed; no int_sop_2 definition was added to the file, and the ul/vl at raygentop.v:2706 belong to a different module and are untouched.

Ports. All eight inputs are connected by name on each instance — clk, reset(1'b0), mode_sigs(12'b0), ax({10'b0,u}) 18b, ay({12'b0,d*u}) 19b, bx({10'b0,v}) 18b, by({12'b0,d*v}) 19b, chainin(37'b0) (tie-off, not chained). result drives only the local res_* wires; chainout() is left unconnected, so nothing reads it. Three blocks of 132 DSP tiles.

Arithmetic. wl = (255-u-v) mod 256, and 256 ≡ 0, 255 ≡ −1 (mod 128), so u*ru+v*rv+wl*rw ≡ u*(ru−rw)+v*(rv−rw)−rw (mod 128); the 7-bit wrapping differences are congruent mod 128 and congruence survives multiplication by u/v, so the folded wl*rw term is kept, not dropped. Block sum ≤ 2·255·127 = 64770, far below 2^37, so res[6:0] is exactly the sum mod 128; the final 7-bit subtract wraps as the original's 15-bit-then-7-bit truncation does (the 15-bit wrap in the original is a multiple of 128, so it is invisible mod 128 — the directed ovf-A/B/C vectors exercise this).

Registers/latency. Original: inputs → stage-1 regs → product regs → combinational sum, 2 edges. Rewrite: block input register replaces stage 1, block result register replaces the product regs and performs the sum, also 2 edges; rwl2 is aligned to the same edge. reset tied low is correct — the module has no reset port and the testbench drives none. Added registers' power-up X clears within 2 edges, inside the 8-cycle lead-in; nothing relies on an initial value.

Visibility/terms/scope. ul,vl,wl,i1*..i3* and the per-colour differences are module-local and read by nothing else; only r,g,b leave the module and their values and cycle alignment are unchanged. No ASSUMES: needed — the identity holds for all input values, consistent with the full-width random stimulus passing.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule
51

prompt

4 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, raygentop.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

id

bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352
