The approved plan

I'll read the source and the measured timing path before deciding.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three 3-product sums of module bilinearintrp: r (2874/2875/2876), g (2877/2878/2879), b (2880/2881/2882)
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • sop_r: ax = {u[6:0], 10'b0, 1'b1} (18 b), ay = {1'b0, nrw, 4'b0, dru} (19 b), bx = {6'b0, 1'b1, 4'b0, v[6:0]} (18 b), by = {1'b0, drv, 10'b0, 1'b1} (19 b), chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk; result -> res_r, chainout unconnected. Output: assign r = res_r[17:11];
  • sop_g: ax = {u[6:0], 10'b0, 1'b1}, ay = {1'b0, ngw, 4'b0, dgu}, bx = {6'b0, 1'b1, 4'b0, v[6:0]}, by = {1'b0, dgv, 10'b0, 1'b1}, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk; result -> res_g. Output: assign g = res_g[17:11];
  • sop_b: ax = {u[6:0], 10'b0, 1'b1}, ay = {1'b0, nbw, 4'b0, dbu}, bx = {6'b0, 1'b1, 4'b0, v[6:0]}, by = {1'b0, dbv, 10'b0, 1'b1}, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk; result -> res_b. Output: assign b = res_b[17:11];
  • new soft signals: dru = ru-rw, drv = rv-rw, dgu = gu-gw, dgv = gv-gw, dbu = bu-bw, dbv = bv-bw (7-bit wrapping subtracts); nrw = ~rw, ngw = ~gw, nbw = ~bw (7-bit bitwise inversions). No soft registers at all.

PLAN:

This is a revision of the kept first pass. Same nine regions, same three blocks, same two register stages; the change is in how each block's four operand ports are packed, and it deletes all the soft logic the kept version needed outside the blocks except the six input subtracts: the three output subtracts and the six 7-bit delay registers (rwl/rwl2, gwl/gwl2, bwl/bwl2 — 42 flip-flops) are gone, replaced by three 7-bit inversions (21 one-input LUTs). The module keeps no register of its own; the block's two register stages are the only ones left.

1. What the original computes (raygentop.v:2762-2887).
u,v (8-bit) are the combinational mux of the module inputs (2818-2858). At each posedge the first stage loads ul<=u, vl<=v, wl<=8'hFF-u-v (8-bit wrapping), rul<=ru … bwl<=bw (2862-2873), and at the same edge the nine products of the previous values of those registers load into i1r…i3b (2874-2882). r,g,b are combinational sums of three products (2884-2886) and are declared [6:0] (2786-2791), so only the low 7 bits of each 3-product sum are visible. With the module inputs sampled at edge k, after edge k+1:
r = (ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl) mod 128 of that same sample, and likewise g, b.

2. The identity (unchanged from the kept pass, re-verified).
wl = (255-u-v) mod 256; a mod-256 reduction differs from the exact value by multiples of 256, which are multiples of 128, so mod 128 wl ≡ 255-u-v ≡ -1-u-v. A product mod 128 depends only on each factor mod 128, so

r ≡ u*(ru-rw) + v*(rv-rw) - rw (mod 128),

with the differences formed as plain 7-bit wrapping subtracts of the raw inputs, and u, v usable as their low 7 bits only. The third product of each colour is folded into the operands, not dropped — that is the regrouping that makes this T2.

3. How the block supplies the -rw term and its 2-cycle delay for free.
The kept pass carried -rw outside the block, which cost a 7-bit subtract on the output and a 2-stage 7-bit delay line (to align rw with the block's 2-cycle result). Instead, pack each operand as a value plus a bit at weight 2^11. With u7 = u[6:0], v7 = v[6:0], nrw = ~rw (7 bits):

  • ax = 2^11*u7 + 1, ay = 2^11*nrw + dru → P1 = 2^22*(u7*nrw) + 2^11*(u7*dru + nrw) + dru
  • bx = 2^11 + v7, by = 2^11*drv + 1 → P2 = 2^22*(drv) + 2^11*(1 + v7*drv) + v7

result = P1 + P2 = 2^22*(u7*nrw + drv) + 2^11*(u7*dru + v7*drv + nrw + 1) + (dru + v7).

  • bits [10:0] hold dru + v7 ≤ 254, so nothing carries into bit 11;
  • bits [17:11] hold (u7*dru + v7*drv + nrw + 1) mod 128, and nrw + 1 = 128 - rw ≡ -rw (mod 128), so result[17:11] is exactly r — no logic after the block;
  • the 2^22 term only adds above bit 21 and never disturbs the field;
  • worst case result = 2^22*(127*127+127) + 2^11*(16129+16129+127+1) + 254 = 68,248,932,606 < 2^37 = 137,438,953,472, so nothing wraps and the bound holds for every input value.

Worked checks against the original expression (all values the same sample):

  • u=200, v=100, ru=10, rv=20, rw=30: original wl=211, (2000+2000+6330) mod 128 = 90. Rewrite: u7=72, v7=100, dru=108, drv=118, nrw=97; field 72*108 + 100*118 + 97 + 1 = 19674, 19674 mod 128 = 90 ✓.
  • u=255, v=255, ru=127, rv=1, rw=63: original wl=1, (32385+255+63) mod 128 = 63. Rewrite: u7=127, v7=127, dru=64, drv=66, nrw=64; 127*64 + 127*66 + 64 + 1 = 16575, mod 128 = 63 ✓.
  • u=v=0, ru=rv=0, rw=127: original (0+0+255*127) mod 128 = 1. Rewrite: dru=drv=1, nrw=0; 0+0+0+1 = 1 ✓.

No ASSUMES: line: the identity and the no-wrap bound hold for every value of the module's inputs, including the testbench's full-width random stimulus.

4. Registers and timing.
Before the rewrite, every converted region has 2 register stages from the module inputs to the output: stage 1 = ul/vl/wl/rul…bwl, stage 2 = the product registers i1r…i3b, then a combinational 2-adder chain to the port. After the rewrite, every converted region still has 2 register stages: the block's input register is stage 1 (it registers u7, v7 and the packed dru/nrw, drv operands) and the block's result register is stage 2, which also performs both products and the sum; the output is a bit slice of that register, with no logic after it. Cycle-for-cycle identical for all nine regions, and strictly shorter than the kept pass on the output side (the kept pass had a 7-bit subtract between the result register and the port).

5. Edits to make in /work/raygentop.v, module bilinearintrp only (lines 2762-2887).

  • Keep the port list, the port declarations, reg[7:0] u; reg[7:0] v; and the whole combinational mux always block (2818-2858) unchanged. Only u[6:0]/v[6:0] are read now, so the mux logic for bit 7 of u and v becomes dead and may be pruned by synthesis; do not edit the mux by hand.
  • Delete the register declarations at 2796-2816 (ul, vl, wl, i1b…i3r, rul, rvl, rwl, gul, gvl, gwl, bul, bvl, bwl) — all nine products, both stages of design registers and the wl subtract disappear.
  • Delete the whole clocked block at 2860-2883. The module is left with no always @(posedge clk); clk is used only by the three block instances.
  • Add:
    wire[6:0] dru = ru - rw; wire[6:0] drv = rv - rw;
    wire[6:0] dgu = gu - gw; wire[6:0] dgv = gv - gw;
    wire[6:0] dbu = bu - bw; wire[6:0] dbv = bv - bw; (7-bit wrapping subtracts — do not widen them)
    wire[6:0] nrw = ~rw; wire[6:0] ngw = ~gw; wire[6:0] nbw = ~bw;
    wire[36:0] res_r, res_g, res_b;
  • Add the three int_sop_2 instances exactly as in MAPPING, connecting every input port by name; chainin tied to 37'b0, mode_sigs to 12'b0, reset to 1'b0; leave each chainout unconnected (.chainout()) — the blocks are not chained and nothing may read it. Check each concatenation's width as written: ax 7+10+1 = 18, ay 1+7+4+7 = 19, bx 6+1+4+7 = 18, by 1+7+10+1 = 19. The single 1'b1 in ax sits at bit 0, the one in bx at bit 11, the one in by at bit 0; u[6:0] sits at ax[17:11], nrw at ay[17:11], v[6:0] at bx[6:0], drv at by[17:11].
  • Replace lines 2884-2886 with assign r = res_r[17:11]; assign g = res_g[17:11]; assign b = res_b[17:11];
  • Do not add a definition of int_sop_2 to raygentop.v (the behavioural model is sim_reference/int_sop_2.v), and change nothing outside module bilinearintrp; the single instance is at line 2012 and its port connections are untouched.

6. Reset and start-up. bilinearintrp has no reset port and the testbench drives none (tb_equiv_bilin.v line 5), so every block's reset is tied low and every block loads at every edge. All block inputs are defined from time 0 (the mux defaults to u=v=0, lines 2852-2856; the operand packing adds only constants and inversions), so every block's result is defined from the second edge, inside the testbench's 8-cycle lead-in. Nothing relies on a power-up value, and with no soft registers left there is nothing else to initialise.

7. Candidates converted and left. All nine candidates are converted; none is left. Six products (2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881) become the blocks' ax*ay and bx*by; the three wl* products (2876, 2879, 2882) are folded into the operands by the mod-128 identity, their -rw/-gw/-bw remainder riding into the block's own result as the nrw + 1 pair of packed addends. Three blocks (3 of 132 DSP tiles, the same count as the kept pass and as the baseline's mapping of this module) replace nine 8×7 multipliers, nine 15-bit product registers, six 15-bit adders, the 8-bit 255-u-v subtract and twelve stage-1 registers; the only soft logic added is six 7-bit subtracts and three 7-bit inversions.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the measured critical path does not run through the converted arithmetic — it starts at a flip-flop upstream of bilinearintrp, crosses two adders (one with a 16-stage carry chain) and three LUT levels of the selectuv mux, and only then reaches the block's input register; 3.8 ns of its 4.87 ns is routing. Registering u01a…v10c/selectuv (or the adders that produce them) one stage earlier in the producing module would cut the period, and no rewrite confined to bilinearintrp can.

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan, the block contract and the correctness rules:

Plan conformance — The edit touches only module bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2762-2907); the harness subtree check (sim_run.log:3) confirms the rest of the file is unchanged, and the instance at raygentop.v:2012 is untouched. The deleted declarations and the whole always @(posedge clk) block match the plan's step 5; the six 7-bit subtracts, three inversions and three wire[36:0] results are exactly those named (2801-2815); three int_sop_2 instances, no soft registers added.

Ports — Every input port is connected by name on all three instances: clk, reset(1'b0), mode_sigs(12'b0), ax, ay, bx, by, chainin(37'b0) (constant drive of the dedicated chain input is permitted). Widths as written: ax 7+10+1=18, ay 1+7+4+7=19, bx 6+1+4+7=18, by 1+7+10+1=19 — all match the port widths. result drives res_r/res_g/res_b, each read only by the output slice; chainout() is left open, so nothing reads it. 3 of 132 DSP tiles.

Arithmetic — Bit positions give ax=2^11·u7+1, ay=2^11·nrw+dru, bx=2^11+v7, by=2^11·drv+1, so result = 2^22(u7·nrw+drv) + 2^11(u7·dru+v7·drv+nrw+1) + (dru+v7). The low field dru+v7 ≤ 254 < 2048 cannot carry into bit 11, the 2^22 term lies above bit 21, and the total ≤ ~6.8e10 < 2^37, so no wrap. Hence result[17:11] = (u7·dru+v7·drv+nrw+1) mod 128, and since nrw+1 = 128−rw ≡ −rw (mod 128), this equals u·ru+v·rv+(255−u−v)·rw mod 128. The original assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) is evaluated in 15 bits (no product truncation: 255·127 = 32385 fits reg[14:0]) and then truncated to the 7-bit output, i.e. mod 128, so the identity reproduces the original exactly for all input values — no ASSUMES: needed. Same for g and b with their own operands.

Registers — Original: inputs sampled at edge k into ul/vl/rul…, products loaded at edge k+1, output combinational from them. Block: operands registered at edge k, result at edge k+1, output a pure bit slice. Identical 2-stage latency, no soft registers added, no reliance on power-up values; the block's two undefined start-up edges fall inside the testbench's 8-cycle lead-in, consistent with the power-up clause.

Visibility and terms — The deleted registers and the nine products were read only inside this module (the ul/vl at 2706-2756 belong to a different module); r, g, b are the only values leaving. All nine product terms are kept — six as the blocks' ax*ay/bx*by, the three wl* products folded into the operands by the mod-128 regrouping, with the −rw/−gw/−bw remainder carried as the (nrw, 1) addends at weight 2^11.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule

prompt

16 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, raygentop.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

id

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or nothing left to try. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.

PLAN:

This is a revision of the kept first pass. Same nine regions, same three blocks, same two register stages; the change is in how each block's four operand ports are packed, and it deletes all the soft logic the kept version needed outside the blocks except the six input subtracts: the three output subtracts and the six 7-bit delay registers (rwl/rwl2, gwl/gwl2, bwl/bwl2 — 42 flip-flops) are gone, replaced by three 7-bit inversions (21 one-input LUTs). The module keeps no register of its own; the block's two register stages are the only ones left.

1. What the original computes (raygentop.v:2762-2887).
u,v (8-bit) are the combinational mux of the module inputs (2818-2858). At each posedge the first stage loads ul<=u, vl<=v, wl<=8'hFF-u-v (8-bit wrapping), rul<=ru … bwl<=bw (2862-2873), and at the same edge the nine products of the previous values of those registers load into i1r…i3b (2874-2882). r,g,b are combinational sums of three products (2884-2886) and are declared [6:0] (2786-2791), so only the low 7 bits of each 3-product sum are visible. With the module inputs sampled at edge k, after edge k+1:
r = (ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl) mod 128 of that same sample, and likewise g, b.

2. The identity (unchanged from the kept pass, re-verified).
wl = (255-u-v) mod 256; a mod-256 reduction differs from the exact value by multiples of 256, which are multiples of 128, so mod 128 wl ≡ 255-u-v ≡ -1-u-v. A product mod 128 depends only on each factor mod 128, so

r ≡ u*(ru-rw) + v*(rv-rw) - rw (mod 128),

with the differences formed as plain 7-bit wrapping subtracts of the raw inputs, and u, v usable as their low 7 bits only. The third product of each colour is folded into the operands, not dropped — that is the regrouping that makes this T2.

3. How the block supplies the -rw term and its 2-cycle delay for free.
The kept pass carried -rw outside the block, which cost a 7-bit subtract on the output and a 2-stage 7-bit delay line (to align rw with the block's 2-cycle result). Instead, pack each operand as a value plus a bit at weight 2^11. With u7 = u[6:0], v7 = v[6:0], nrw = ~rw (7 bits):

  • ax = 2^11*u7 + 1, ay = 2^11*nrw + dru → P1 = 2^22*(u7*nrw) + 2^11*(u7*dru + nrw) + dru
  • bx = 2^11 + v7, by = 2^11*drv + 1 → P2 = 2^22*(drv) + 2^11*(1 + v7*drv) + v7

result = P1 + P2 = 2^22*(u7*nrw + drv) + 2^11*(u7*dru + v7*drv + nrw + 1) + (dru + v7).

  • bits [10:0] hold dru + v7 ≤ 254, so nothing carries into bit 11;
  • bits [17:11] hold (u7*dru + v7*drv + nrw + 1) mod 128, and nrw + 1 = 128 - rw ≡ -rw (mod 128), so result[17:11] is exactly r — no logic after the block;
  • the 2^22 term only adds above bit 21 and never disturbs the field;
  • worst case result = 2^22*(127*127+127) + 2^11*(16129+16129+127+1) + 254 = 68,248,932,606 < 2^37 = 137,438,953,472, so nothing wraps and the bound holds for every input value.

Worked checks against the original expression (all values the same sample):

  • u=200, v=100, ru=10, rv=20, rw=30: original wl=211, (2000+2000+6330) mod 128 = 90. Rewrite: u7=72, v7=100, dru=108, drv=118, nrw=97; field 72*108 + 100*118 + 97 + 1 = 19674, 19674 mod 128 = 90 ✓.
  • u=255, v=255, ru=127, rv=1, rw=63: original wl=1, (32385+255+63) mod 128 = 63. Rewrite: u7=127, v7=127, dru=64, drv=66, nrw=64; 127*64 + 127*66 + 64 + 1 = 16575, mod 128 = 63 ✓.
  • u=v=0, ru=rv=0, rw=127: original (0+0+255*127) mod 128 = 1. Rewrite: dru=drv=1, nrw=0; 0+0+0+1 = 1 ✓.

No ASSUMES: line: the identity and the no-wrap bound hold for every value of the module's inputs, including the testbench's full-width random stimulus.

4. Registers and timing.
Before the rewrite, every converted region has 2 register stages from the module inputs to the output: stage 1 = ul/vl/wl/rul…bwl, stage 2 = the product registers i1r…i3b, then a combinational 2-adder chain to the port. After the rewrite, every converted region still has 2 register stages: the block's input register is stage 1 (it registers u7, v7 and the packed dru/nrw, drv operands) and the block's result register is stage 2, which also performs both products and the sum; the output is a bit slice of that register, with no logic after it. Cycle-for-cycle identical for all nine regions, and strictly shorter than the kept pass on the output side (the kept pass had a 7-bit subtract between the result register and the port).

5. Edits to make in /work/raygentop.v, module bilinearintrp only (lines 2762-2887).

  • Keep the port list, the port declarations, reg[7:0] u; reg[7:0] v; and the whole combinational mux always block (2818-2858) unchanged. Only u[6:0]/v[6:0] are read now, so the mux logic for bit 7 of u and v becomes dead and may be pruned by synthesis; do not edit the mux by hand.
  • Delete the register declarations at 2796-2816 (ul, vl, wl, i1b…i3r, rul, rvl, rwl, gul, gvl, gwl, bul, bvl, bwl) — all nine products, both stages of design registers and the wl subtract disappear.
  • Delete the whole clocked block at 2860-2883. The module is left with no always @(posedge clk); clk is used only by the three block instances.
  • Add:
    wire[6:0] dru = ru - rw; wire[6:0] drv = rv - rw;
    wire[6:0] dgu = gu - gw; wire[6:0] dgv = gv - gw;
    wire[6:0] dbu = bu - bw; wire[6:0] dbv = bv - bw; (7-bit wrapping subtracts — do not widen them)
    wire[6:0] nrw = ~rw; wire[6:0] ngw = ~gw; wire[6:0] nbw = ~bw;
    wire[36:0] res_r, res_g, res_b;
  • Add the three int_sop_2 instances exactly as in MAPPING, connecting every input port by name; chainin tied to 37'b0, mode_sigs to 12'b0, reset to 1'b0; leave each chainout unconnected (.chainout()) — the blocks are not chained and nothing may read it. Check each concatenation's width as written: ax 7+10+1 = 18, ay 1+7+4+7 = 19, bx 6+1+4+7 = 18, by 1+7+10+1 = 19. The single 1'b1 in ax sits at bit 0, the one in bx at bit 11, the one in by at bit 0; u[6:0] sits at ax[17:11], nrw at ay[17:11], v[6:0] at bx[6:0], drv at by[17:11].
  • Replace lines 2884-2886 with assign r = res_r[17:11]; assign g = res_g[17:11]; assign b = res_b[17:11];
  • Do not add a definition of int_sop_2 to raygentop.v (the behavioural model is sim_reference/int_sop_2.v), and change nothing outside module bilinearintrp; the single instance is at line 2012 and its port connections are untouched.

6. Reset and start-up. bilinearintrp has no reset port and the testbench drives none (tb_equiv_bilin.v line 5), so every block's reset is tied low and every block loads at every edge. All block inputs are defined from time 0 (the mux defaults to u=v=0, lines 2852-2856; the operand packing adds only constants and inversions), so every block's result is defined from the second edge, inside the testbench's 8-cycle lead-in. Nothing relies on a power-up value, and with no soft registers left there is nothing else to initialise.

7. Candidates converted and left. All nine candidates are converted; none is left. Six products (2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881) become the blocks' ax*ay and bx*by; the three wl* products (2876, 2879, 2882) are folded into the operands by the mod-128 identity, their -rw/-gw/-bw remainder riding into the block's own result as the nrw + 1 pair of packed addends. Three blocks (3 of 132 DSP tiles, the same count as the kept pass and as the baseline's mapping of this module) replace nine 8×7 multipliers, nine 15-bit product registers, six 15-bit adders, the 8-bit 255-u-v subtract and twelve stage-1 registers; the only soft logic added is six 7-bit subtracts and three 7-bit inversions.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the measured critical path does not run through the converted arithmetic — it starts at a flip-flop upstream of bilinearintrp, crosses two adders (one with a 16-stage carry chain) and three LUT levels of the selectuv mux, and only then reaches the block's input register; 3.8 ns of its 4.87 ns is routing. Registering u01a…v10c/selectuv (or the adders that produce them) one stage earlier in the producing module would cut the period, and no rewrite confined to bilinearintrp can.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: raygentop.v

response

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: NONE
REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
MAPPING: NONE
PLAN:

I propose no further rewrite this pass. The reason is "nothing left to try": the kept candidate (revision 1) already converts all nine candidate regions onto three blocks, that block count is the structural minimum for this arithmetic, and the measured critical path no longer runs through any arithmetic a rewrite confined to bilinearintrp can touch.

1. Status of every candidate region. All nine candidates — bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348 through …:2882$356 — are already converted by the kept candidate (revision 1), which stands as the result to keep. None is left for a correctness reason; there is simply no tenth region and no unconverted product. This pass adds no block and changes nothing: blocks added = 0.

2. Register stages, unchanged from the kept rewrite. For each of the nine regions: before the rewrite, 2 stages from the module inputs to r/g/b (stage 1 = ul/vl/wl/rul…bwl at raygentop.v:2862-2873, stage 2 = the product registers i1r…i3b at raygentop.v:2874-2882, then a combinational 2-adder chain at raygentop.v:2884-2886). After the rewrite, still 2 stages: the block's operand register is stage 1 and its result register is stage 2, with the output a bit slice (result[17:11]) and no logic after it. That is the minimum the block permits (it has 2 registers and no bypass around either), so no further stage can be removed, and none can be added without changing the module's latency.

3. Why three blocks is the floor, so no block can be removed for area. Mod 128, wl = (255-u-v) mod 256 ≡ -1-u-v, so each colour needs u7*(ru-rw) + v7*(rv-rw) - rw — six 7×7 products in all, plus three constant-ish terms that the kept packing already carries free inside the block's own operands (nrw + 1 at weight 2^11). A single ax*ay of this block cannot host two useful 7×7 products: two 7-bit values in ax can be spaced at most 11 bits (18-bit port), while each partial product is 14 bits wide and a sum of two is 15, so the low cross term always carries into the field that holds the wanted sum. Cancelling that cross term (which is possible, by putting (-dru) mod 2^19 on the other operand and reading only bits below 19, since the compensating 2^19·v7 is ≡ 0 mod 128 after the shift) consumes the block's second product, so a block still yields exactly one colour. Six products therefore need six product slots, i.e. three blocks, which is what the kept rewrite instantiates. The measured DSP count (6 tiles) already equals the baseline's.

4. Why chaining cannot be used to pack or to force adjacency. chainin is registered at the block's entry, so a contribution entering block n+1 through the chain reaches its result one cycle after that block's own operands do. Aligning them would require feeding the upstream block its data one cycle earlier, i.e. a third register stage on the chained colour, which breaks the module's 2-cycle latency. Chaining is therefore unusable here even purely as a placement constraint, and each block's chainin must stay tied to 37'b0 with chainout unconnected, as in the kept rewrite.

5. Why no further delay is available inside the region. At seed 1 the 4.30 ns path starts at a flip-flop upstream of bilinearintrp, crosses an adder carry chain and three LUT levels of the selectuv mux, and ends at a block operand register (setup 0.019); about 3.2 ns of it is routing. The block's own stage is 2.219 ns and the result clock-to-Q path is not critical, so the binding path is entirely in logic the rewrite does not own. The mux output must feed the block's operand register directly — pushing the mux behind a soft register would make the block's two registers stages 2 and 3 and change the latency — so there is no legal restructuring inside this module that shortens it. The one remaining variant I weighed (splitting the six products so each block holds two products of the same multiplicand, which would drop u's and v's fan-out from three DSP tiles to two) requires three 7-bit mod-128 adders after the block results: it adds soft area and output-path delay for a speculative routing gain, so its expected effect on area × delay is not positive.

6. What this pass therefore keeps. Revision 1, as built and measured: area 4.59037e+06, period 4.30023 ns at seed 1, product 1.97396e+07, −14.40% against the baseline median, with A/A0 0.965 and D/D0 0.887. Its margin is well outside the ~4.6% seed spread of the unmodified design, and no soft registers or output-side logic remain in the converted module for a later pass to remove. Proposing a cosmetic re-packing now would only re-roll placement noise at seed 1 and risks displacing a candidate whose all-seed median is better.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the period is set by a path outside bilinearintrp — upstream flip-flop → adder (16-stage carry chain) → three LUT levels of the selectuv mux → the block's operand register, roughly 3.2 ns of it routing. Registering u01a…v10c / selectuv one stage earlier in the producing module (or registering the adders that produce them) would cut the period for both the original and the rewritten design; the same change would also let the mux sit after a register, shortening what the block's operand register must wait for. No rewrite confined to bilinearintrp can do this, because the module's 2-cycle latency leaves no stage to spend on the mux.

+ reg[7:0] u;
+ reg[7:0] v;
+
+ // Operand packing for the three int_sop_2 blocks.
+ // Mod 128, wl = (255-u-v) mod 256 == -1-u-v, so
+ // r == u[6:0]*(ru-rw) + v[6:0]*(rv-rw) - rw (mod 128),
+ // with every subtract taken 7 bits wide (wrapping).
+ wire[6:0] dru = ru - rw;
+ wire[6:0] drv = rv - rw;
+ wire[6:0] dgu = gu - gw;
+ wire[6:0] dgv = gv - gw;
+ wire[6:0] dbu = bu - bw;
+ wire[6:0] dbv = bv - bw;
+ // ~rw + 1 == 128 - rw == -rw (mod 128): the third product's remainder rides
+ // into the block's own sum as the pair (nrw, 1) at weight 2^11.
+ wire[6:0] nrw = ~rw;
+ wire[6:0] ngw = ~gw;
+ wire[6:0] nbw = ~bw;
+
+ wire[36:0] res_r;
+ wire[36:0] res_g;
+ wire[36:0] res_b;
always @(selectuv or u01a or u01b or u01c or v01a or v01b or v01c or u10a or
u10b or u10c or v10a or v10b or v10c)

+ // ax = 2^11*u7 + 1, ay = 2^11*nrw + dru, bx = 2^11 + v7, by = 2^11*drv + 1
+ // => result = 2^22*(u7*nrw + drv)
+ // + 2^11*(u7*dru + v7*drv + nrw + 1)
+ // + (dru + v7)
+ // dru + v7 <= 254 never carries into bit 11, and the whole sum is < 2^37,
+ // so result[17:11] == (u7*dru + v7*drv - rw) mod 128 == r.
+ int_sop_2 sop_r (
+ .clk(clk),
+ .reset(1'b0),
+ .mode_sigs(12'b0),
+ .ax({u[6:0], 10'b0, 1'b1}),
+ .ay({1'b0, nrw, 4'b0, dru}),
+ .bx({6'b0, 1'b1, 4'b0, v[6:0]}),
+ .by({1'b0, drv, 10'b0, 1'b1}),
+ .chainin(37'b0),
+ .result(res_r),
+ .chainout()
+ );
+
+ int_sop_2 sop_g (
+ .clk(clk),
+ .reset(1'b0),
+ .mode_sigs(12'b0),
+ .ax({u[6:0], 10'b0, 1'b1}),
+ .ay({1'b0, ngw, 4'b0, dgu}),
+ .bx({6'b0, 1'b1, 4'b0, v[6:0]}),
+ .by({1'b0, dgv, 10'b0, 1'b1}),
+ .chainin(37'b0),
+ .result(res_g),
+ .chainout()
+ );
+
+ int_sop_2 sop_b (
+ .clk(clk),
+ .reset(1'b0),
+ .mode_sigs(12'b0),
+ .ax({u[6:0], 10'b0, 1'b1}),
+ .ay({1'b0, nbw, 4'b0, dbu}),
+ .bx({6'b0, 1'b1, 4'b0, v[6:0]}),
+ .by({1'b0, dbv, 10'b0, 1'b1}),
+ .chainin(37'b0),
+ .result(res_b),
+ .chainout()
+ );

Objective loop

2 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: planner declined
revisions allowed
9
seeds per rewrite
1, 2, 3, 4, 5 (adaptive)

Pass 2
loop stopped by
planner declined (no representative mapping)
objective
area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02)
baseline
layout koios_extra_small · used area 4.7578e+06 · critical path 4.84712 ns · wirelength 25664 · area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02) 2.30616e+07 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02): A times D, from VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded), A, the delay D (the register-to-register figure since 2026-10-20) and the total routed wirelength, W (all three recorded under every objective). Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the objective is formed from the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 4.60%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 4.60%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.

+ reg[7:0] u;

+ // Operand packing for the three int_sop_2 blocks.
+ // Mod 128, wl = (255-u-v) mod 256 == -1-u-v, so
+ // r == u[6:0]*(ru-rw) + v[6:0]*(rv-rw) - rw (mod 128),
+ // with every subtract taken 7 bits wide (wrapping).
+ wire[6:0] dru = ru - rw;
+ wire[6:0] drv = rv - rw;
+ wire[6:0] dgu = gu - gw;
+ wire[6:0] dgv = gv - gw;
+ wire[6:0] dbu = bu - bw;
+ wire[6:0] dbv = bv - bw;
+ // ~rw + 1 == 128 - rw == -rw (mod 128): the third product's remainder rides
+ // into the block's own sum as the pair (nrw, 1) at weight 2^11.
+ wire[6:0] nrw = ~rw;
+ wire[6:0] ngw = ~gw;
+ wire[6:0] nbw = ~bw;

- rul <= ru ;
- rvl <= rv ;
- rwl <= rw ;
- gul <= gu ;
- gvl <= gv ;
- gwl <= gw ;
- bul <= bu ;
- bvl <= bv ;
- bwl <= bw ;
- i1r <= ul * rul ;
- i2r <= vl * rvl ;
- i3r <= wl * rwl ;
- i1g <= ul * gul ;
- i2g <= vl * gvl ;
- i3g <= wl * gwl ;
- i1b <= ul * bul ;
- i2b <= vl * bvl ;
- i3b <= wl * bwl ;
- end
- assign r = (i1r + i2r + i3r) ;
- assign g = (i1g + i2g + i3g) ;
- assign b = (i1b + i2b + i3b) ;
+ // ax = 2^11*u7 + 1, ay = 2^11*nrw + dru, bx = 2^11 + v7, by = 2^11*drv + 1
+ // => result = 2^22*(u7*nrw + drv)
+ // + 2^11*(u7*dru + v7*drv + nrw + 1)
+ // + (dru + v7)
+ // dru + v7 <= 254 never carries into bit 11, and the whole sum is < 2^37,
+ // so result[17:11] == (u7*dru + v7*drv - rw) mod 128 == r.
+ int_sop_2 sop_r (
+ .clk(clk),
+ .reset(1'b0),
+ .mode_sigs(12'b0),
+ .ax({u[6:0], 10'b0, 1'b1}),
+ .ay({1'b0, nrw, 4'b0, dru}),
+ .bx({6'b0, 1'b1, 4'b0, v[6:0]}),
+ .by({1'b0, drv, 10'b0, 1'b1}),
+ .chainin(37'b0),
+ .result(res_r),
+ .chainout()
+ );
+
+ int_sop_2 sop_g (
+ .clk(clk),
+ .reset(1'b0),
+ .mode_sigs(12'b0),
+ .ax({u[6:0], 10'b0, 1'b1}),
+ .ay({1'b0, ngw, 4'b0, dgu}),
+ .bx({6'b0, 1'b1, 4'b0, v[6:0]}),
+ .by({1'b0, dgv, 10'b0, 1'b1}),
+ .chainin(37'b0),
+ .result(res_g),
+ .chainout()
+ );
+
+ int_sop_2 sop_b (
+ .clk(clk),
+ .reset(1'b0),
+ .mode_sigs(12'b0),
+ .ax({u[6:0], 10'b0, 1'b1}),
+ .ay({1'b0, nbw, 4'b0, dbu}),
+ .bx({6'b0, 1'b1, 4'b0, v[6:0]}),
+ .by({1'b0, dbv, 10'b0, 1'b1}),
+ .chainin(37'b0),
+ .result(res_b),
+ .chainout()
+ );
+
+ assign r = res_r[17:11] ;
+ assign g = res_g[17:11] ;
+ assign b = res_b[17:11] ;
endmodule

TIER: NONE
REGIONS: NONE

PLAN:

I propose no further rewrite this pass. The reason is "nothing left to try": the kept candidate (revision 1) already converts all nine candidate regions onto three blocks, that block count is the structural minimum for this arithmetic, and the measured critical path no longer runs through any arithmetic a rewrite confined to bilinearintrp can touch.

1. Status of every candidate region. All nine candidates — bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348 through …:2882$356 — are already converted by the kept candidate (revision 1), which stands as the result to keep. None is left for a correctness reason; there is simply no tenth region and no unconverted product. This pass adds no block and changes nothing: blocks added = 0.

2. Register stages, unchanged from the kept rewrite. For each of the nine regions: before the rewrite, 2 stages from the module inputs to r/g/b (stage 1 = ul/vl/wl/rul…bwl at raygentop.v:2862-2873, stage 2 = the product registers i1r…i3b at raygentop.v:2874-2882, then a combinational 2-adder chain at raygentop.v:2884-2886). After the rewrite, still 2 stages: the block's operand register is stage 1 and its result register is stage 2, with the output a bit slice (result[17:11]) and no logic after it. That is the minimum the block permits (it has 2 registers and no bypass around either), so no further stage can be removed, and none can be added without changing the module's latency.

3. Why three blocks is the floor, so no block can be removed for area. Mod 128, wl = (255-u-v) mod 256 ≡ -1-u-v, so each colour needs u7*(ru-rw) + v7*(rv-rw) - rw — six 7×7 products in all, plus three constant-ish terms that the kept packing already carries free inside the block's own operands (nrw + 1 at weight 2^11). A single ax*ay of this block cannot host two useful 7×7 products: two 7-bit values in ax can be spaced at most 11 bits (18-bit port), while each partial product is 14 bits wide and a sum of two is 15, so the low cross term always carries into the field that holds the wanted sum. Cancelling that cross term (which is possible, by putting (-dru) mod 2^19 on the other operand and reading only bits below 19, since the compensating 2^19·v7 is ≡ 0 mod 128 after the shift) consumes the block's second product, so a block still yields exactly one colour. Six products therefore need six product slots, i.e. three blocks, which is what the kept rewrite instantiates. The measured DSP count (6 tiles) already equals the baseline's.

4. Why chaining cannot be used to pack or to force adjacency. chainin is registered at the block's entry, so a contribution entering block n+1 through the chain reaches its result one cycle after that block's own operands do. Aligning them would require feeding the upstream block its data one cycle earlier, i.e. a third register stage on the chained colour, which breaks the module's 2-cycle latency. Chaining is therefore unusable here even purely as a placement constraint, and each block's chainin must stay tied to 37'b0 with chainout unconnected, as in the kept rewrite.

5. Why no further delay is available inside the region. At seed 1 the 4.30 ns path starts at a flip-flop upstream of bilinearintrp, crosses an adder carry chain and three LUT levels of the selectuv mux, and ends at a block operand register (setup 0.019); about 3.2 ns of it is routing. The block's own stage is 2.219 ns and the result clock-to-Q path is not critical, so the binding path is entirely in logic the rewrite does not own. The mux output must feed the block's operand register directly — pushing the mux behind a soft register would make the block's two registers stages 2 and 3 and change the latency — so there is no legal restructuring inside this module that shortens it. The one remaining variant I weighed (splitting the six products so each block holds two products of the same multiplicand, which would drop u's and v's fan-out from three DSP tiles to two) requires three 7-bit mod-128 adders after the block results: it adds soft area and output-path delay for a speculative routing gain, so its expected effect on area × delay is not positive.

6. What this pass therefore keeps. Revision 1, as built and measured: area 4.59037e+06, period 4.30023 ns at seed 1, product 1.97396e+07, −14.40% against the baseline median, with A/A0 0.965 and D/D0 0.887. Its margin is well outside the ~4.6% seed spread of the unmodified design, and no soft registers or output-side logic remain in the converted module for a later pass to remove. Proposing a cosmetic re-packing now would only re-roll placement noise at seed 1 and risks displacing a candidate whose all-seed median is better.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the period is set by a path outside bilinearintrp — upstream flip-flop → adder (16-stage carry chain) → three LUT levels of the selectuv mux → the block's operand register, roughly 3.2 ns of it routing. Registering u01a…v10c / selectuv one stage earlier in the producing module (or registering the adders that produce them) would cut the period for both the original and the rewritten design; the same change would also let the mux sit after a register, shortening what the block's operand register must wait for. No rewrite confined to bilinearintrp can do this, because the module's 2-cycle latency leaves no stage to spend on the mux.
